<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

- Discovery: The list above is the skills available in this session (name + description + file path). Skill bodies live on disk at the listed paths.

  1) After deciding to use a skill, open its `SKILL.md`. Read only enough to follow the workflow.
  2) When `SKILL.md` references relative paths (e.g., `scripts/foo.py`), resolve them relative to the skill directory listed above first, and only consider other paths if needed.
  3) If `SKILL.md` points to extra folders such as `references/`, load only the specific files needed for the request; don't bulk-load everything.
  4) If `scripts/` exist, prefer running or patching them instead of retyping large code blocks.
  5) If `assets/` or templates exist, reuse them instead of recreating from scratch.
- Coordination and sequencing:
  - If multiple skills apply, choose the minimal set that covers the request and state the order you'll use them.
  - Announce which skill(s) you're using and why (one short line). If you skip an obvious skill, say why.
- Context hygiene:
  - Keep context small: summarize long sections instead of pasting them; only load extra files when needed.
  - Avoid deep reference-chasing: prefer opening only files directly linked from `SKILL.md` unless you're blocked.
  - When variants exist (frameworks, providers, domains), pick only the relevant reference file(s) and note that choice.
- Safety and fallback: If a skill can't be applied cleanly (missing files, unclear instructions), state the issue, pick the next-best approach, and continue.

- 根因在这层：`generate_subtitles` 没有时间戳有效性校验，而且分句逻辑是“先按 gap 判切，再补 token”，对重复词/异常时间戳很容易产出零时长行。  
- 后面翻译、TTS、合并只是吃这份坏的 `source.srt`；`final` 只是加了全局 offset，所以“看起来时间轴对”，但配音已经被前面污染了。  

结论一句话：**源头是 ASR→source.srt 生成步骤坏了，不是后处理坏了。**

核心原因：**上游 ASR 给了坏时间戳，你的代码把坏时间戳原样放大了**。

具体是这三层叠加：

- **第 1 层（源头）**：`segment_0001` 这段音频里大量是短促重复语气词（如 “oh, yeah / oh, fuck”），ASR/对齐器在这种语料上容易把多个 token 压成同一时间点（你这次直接出现大量 `0 -> 0`）。
- **第 2 层（放大器）**：`src/subtitle_maker/transcriber.py` 的 `generate_subtitles`（`src/subtitle_maker/transcriber.py:204`）没有做“时间戳有效性检查”（比如 `end > start`、单调递增、最小时长），所以坏时间戳直接写进 `source.srt`。
- **第 3 层（全链路传染）**：`tools/dub_pipeline.py` 后续翻译/TTS/对齐都依赖这份 `source.srt` 的时长（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:314`），所以前面一坏，后面全错。

你看到“final 看着对”的原因：  
- `tools/dub_long_video.py` 合并时只是在段内时间上加偏移（`tools/dub_long_video.py:255`），**不会修复段内坏时间戳**。所以是“表面时间轴对，底层配音依然错”。

一句话：**不是切段错、不是 merge 错，是 ASR 时间戳在第一段退化后，缺少源头校验与纠正导致全链路污染。**

**OpenAI Codex**

你说得对，本质上都是“识别字幕”，但**两条链路输入条件不一样**，所以会出现“2. Generate Subtitles 正常、Auto Dubbing 出错”。

关键差异（有代码出处）：

- `2. Generate Subtitles` 走 `web.py -> transcribe_task()`，用的是**分块识别**：`transcribe_iter(..., chunk_size=30)`，每 30 秒一块再 `generate_subtitles`  
  见 `src/subtitle_maker/web.py:130`、`src/subtitle_maker/web.py:155`、`src/subtitle_maker/web.py:165`。
- Auto Dubbing 走 `dub_pipeline.py -> load_or_transcribe_subtitles()`，用的是**整段识别**：`generator.transcribe(vocals_audio)`，然后一次性 `generate_subtitles`  
  见 `tools/dub_pipeline.py:314`、`tools/dub_pipeline.py:344`、`tools/dub_pipeline.py:345`。
- Auto Dubbing 识别输入是**分离后的人声 stem**（不是原始音轨），来源于 `separate_audio()`  
  见 `tools/dub_pipeline.py:3670` 附近与 `separate_audio` 调用链。
- 两条链路都最终用同一个 `generate_subtitles()`，但这个函数**没有时间戳守卫**（零时长/逆序不会被拦）  
  见 `src/subtitle_maker/transcriber.py:204`。

所以“为啥会错”不是“识别不是识别”，而是：
**Auto Dubbing 的前置输入（长静音 + stem 特性）+ 整段识别方式，更容易触发坏时间戳；而当前 `generate_subtitles` 又不会拦截坏时间戳。**  
这就是源头。

**User**

为啥auto dubbing要这样处理呢

---


**OpenAI Codex**

因为 `auto dubbing` 的目标和 `2. Generate Subtitles` 不一样，它要的是“可配音产物”，所以当初才这么设计：

- 先做人声分离（`source_vocals`），是为了后面克隆音色、提参考音、混回 BGM（这是配音链路刚需）。
- 然后在 `dub_pipeline` 里一条龙跑完：识别→翻译→TTS→对齐→混音→产物清单，便于任务编排和失败恢复。
- 长视频外层再切段（`dub_long_video`），是为了控制显存/耗时并支持断点续跑。

问题不在“为什么要这样设计”，而在**这版实现缺了防线**：
- 在 `auto dubbing` 里用了整段识别+stem 输入，但没有对 ASR 时间戳做健康检查；
- 一旦出现坏时间戳（零时长/逆序），就直接进入后续配音流程，导致你看到“段内全错”。

所以：架构动机是合理的，当前 bug 是“工程防护没补齐”。

---


**OpenAI Codex**

你说的“理论上”完全对：  
`source_vocals` 正常应与原音频等长，只是去掉伴奏。

但“等长”只保证**全局时长**，不保证**局部时间戳稳定**。会出错的常见原因是：

- 分离模型会引入局部失真/门限效应（弱音、气声、辅音被压掉或拉扁）。
- 长静音 + 短促重复词（你这个样本就是）最容易让 ASR 对齐器把多个词压到同一时刻。
- 现在流程里没有时间戳守卫，坏时间戳（如 `0->0`）直接进了 SRT。

所以不是“人声分离一定导致错”，而是：  
**人声分离后的音频分布更极端时，ASR 时间戳更容易退化；没有校验就会出事故。**

**OpenAI Codex**

**Spec-2 功能点（规划）**

- 目标一：`auto dubbing` 支持 `time_ranges`（手动指定区间）。
- 目标二：支持“自动选择要配音区间”（默认跳过长静音段）。
- 目标三：区间内全流程生效（ASR/翻译/TTS/混音/SRT），区间外不配音。
- 目标四：最终 `final` 保持全局时间轴一致（可与原视频对齐）。

**接口与数据结构**
- Web 入参新增：`time_ranges`（JSON 数组，元素 `{start_sec, end_sec}`）。
- Web 入参新增：`auto_pick_ranges`（bool，默认 false）。
- Web 入参新增：`auto_pick_min_silence_sec`、`auto_pick_min_speech_sec`（阈值可调）。
- CLI 层统一为：`--time-ranges-json` + `--auto-pick-ranges` + 两个阈值参数。
- `batch_manifest.json` 增加：`requested_ranges`、`effective_ranges`、`range_strategy`。

**执行语义**
- `time_ranges` 有值：优先手动区间。
- `time_ranges` 为空且 `auto_pick_ranges=true`：自动检测语音活跃区间。
- 两者都无：保持当前全量行为（兼容）。
- 多段视频时，区间先映射到原视频时间轴，再切到各 segment 本地时间轴执行。

**产物规则**
- `source/translated/dubbed_final.srt` 仅包含处理区间内字幕，时间戳仍为原视频绝对时间。
- 音频混合仅替换处理区间；非处理区间保留原轨。
- `manual_review` 增加 `out_of_selected_ranges_skipped` 统计（可观测）。

**验证口径**
- 手动区间：只在指定片段出现配音。
- 自动区间：前导长静音不进入配音。
- 无区间：行为与当前版本一致。
- `final` 时间轴连续可对齐视频。

如果这段你确认，我继续给 **Spec-3 风险与决策**。

**OpenAI Codex**

**Spec-3 风险与决策**

- 风险1：区间边界切得太硬，句子被截断。  
  - 决策：区间与字幕做“整句吸附”（落到完整字幕边界），默认边界缓冲 `±0.2s`。

- 风险2：自动区间误检（把弱语音当静音）。  
  - 决策：仅作为“默认建议区间”，保留手动覆盖；manifest 记录 `requested/effective` 便于回放。

- 风险3：长视频分段后区间映射错误。  
  - 决策：统一用“原视频绝对时间轴”存区间，进入每个 segment 时再做局部裁剪映射。

- 风险4：区间外音频处理不一致导致听感跳变。  
  - 决策：区间外保留原音轨，区间边界加短淡入淡出（如 30~80ms）。

- 风险5：兼容性回归（旧前端/旧调用）。  
  - 决策：所有新参数可选，默认全量流程不变；无参数即旧行为。

- 风险6：可观测性不足，线上难排查。  
  - 决策：新增日志事件：`range_selection_started`、`range_selection_result`、`range_mapping_applied`、`range_outside_skipped`。

如果你确认，我下一步就按这份规划开始正式实现。

<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

**User**

不是不要压，是在压之前想多一步，看看当前时间戳尾部和下一句话时间戳的开头的时间是否能够补回来，如果这个区间是不说话的，可以不用压缩的，你明白我意思吗？

---


**OpenAI Codex**

明白，你这个思路非常对。  

你要的是：

- **先不急着压缩**  
- 先看“当前句结束 -> 下一句开始”之间有没有**静音缓冲**  
- 如果有缓冲，就把这段缓冲借给当前句，**允许当前句更长**，只要不撞到下一句就行  
- 只有仍然超出可用窗口时，才做 `sentence_fit` 压缩

我建议落地规则：

1. `target = 当前句原时间窗`
2. `gap = next_start - current_end`（只取 `>0`）
3. `allowed = target + max(0, gap - guard)`（`guard` 预留 80~120ms 防止贴边）
4. 若 `raw_duration <= allowed`：不压缩（保留自然语速）  
5. 若 `raw_duration > allowed`：压到 `allowed`（而不是直接压到 `target`）

这样会明显减少“2.67s 被硬压到 1.2s”这种听感问题，同时不破坏后续句子的时间戳边界。

要我现在就按这个规则改到 `sentence` 分支里吗？

---


**User**

4，5在解析一下

---


**OpenAI Codex**

可以，4 和 5 是这套策略的“决策分叉”：

- **4）`raw_duration <= allowed`：不压缩**
  - `raw_duration` = 当前句 TTS 实际时长
  - `allowed` = 当前句可用上限（原窗口 + 可借静音）
  - 含义：当前句即使比原字幕长一点，但还没撞到下一句可用边界，就**保留原语速**，不做 fit。

- **5）`raw_duration > allowed`：压到 `allowed`**
  - 含义：当前句已经会侵占下一句，就必须压。
  - 但不是压回原始 `target`，而是压到更宽松的 `allowed`，尽量少压，保留自然度。

**例子（你的场景）**  
- 原窗口 `target=1.2s`，下一句前有 `gap=1.6s` 静音，`guard=0.1s`  
- `allowed = 1.2 + (1.6-0.1)=2.7s`  
- 若 `raw=2.67s`：满足第4条，不压  
- 若 `raw=3.1s`：走第5条，压到 `2.7s`（而不是压到 `1.2s`）

<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

- Discovery: The list above is the skills available in this session (name + description + file path). Skill bodies live on disk at the listed paths.

- How to use a skill (progressive disclosure):
  1) After deciding to use a skill, open its `SKILL.md`. Read only enough to follow the workflow.
  2) When `SKILL.md` references relative paths (e.g., `scripts/foo.py`), resolve them relative to the skill directory listed above first, and only consider other paths if needed.
  3) If `SKILL.md` points to extra folders such as `references/`, load only the specific files needed for the request; don't bulk-load everything.

  - If multiple skills apply, choose the minimal set that covers the request and state the order you'll use them.
  - Announce which skill(s) you're using and why (one short line). If you skip an obvious skill, say why.
- Context hygiene:

  - Avoid deep reference-chasing: prefer opening only files directly linked from `SKILL.md` unless you're blocked.
  - When variants exist (frameworks, providers, domains), pick only the relevant reference file(s) and note that choice.
- Safety and fallback: If a skill can't be applied cleanly (missing files, unclear instructions), state the issue, pick the next-best approach, and continue.

**OpenAI Codex**

你这个现象有两个原因：

- 现在“**一句话一句话**”只在你上传 `translated` 字幕模式里强制生效；  
  其他模式（ASR/源字幕）还会走 `grouped_synthesis`，会把多句合成一组。
- 就算是单句，`index-tts` 内部也会按 token 把长句切成 `part000/part001...` 再拼回去，这是模型内部切分，不是字幕切分。

所以你看到“零碎片段”不一定代表没按句处理。  
如果你要**所有模式**都强制一行一行，我可以直接改成全局 `grouped_synthesis=false`。

**OpenAI Codex**

<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

### How to use skills
- Discovery: The list above is the skills available in this session (name + description + file path). Skill bodies live on disk at the listed paths.

- How to use a skill (progressive disclosure):
  1) After deciding to use a skill, open its `SKILL.md`. Read only enough to follow the workflow.
  2) When `SKILL.md` references relative paths (e.g., `scripts/foo.py`), resolve them relative to the skill directory listed above first, and only consider other paths if needed.
  3) If `SKILL.md` points to extra folders such as `references/`, load only the specific files needed for the request; don't bulk-load everything.
  4) If `scripts/` exist, prefer running or patching them instead of retyping large code blocks.

  - Announce which skill(s) you're using and why (one short line). If you skip an obvious skill, say why.
- Context hygiene:

  - When variants exist (frameworks, providers, domains), pick only the relevant reference file(s) and note that choice.
- Safety and fallback: If a skill can't be applied cleanly (missing files, unclear instructions), state the issue, pick the next-best approach, and continue.

我和chatgpt讨论了本项目，它给我的回复我觉得有道理：你这个现象我直接给你定性一下：
👉 **不是 forced alignment 出问题，而是“生成端 + 对齐端不一致”导致的节奏撕裂。**

换句话说：

> 你在“事后对齐一个本来就节奏不稳定的语音”
> 👉 那结果一定是：忽快忽慢、卡顿、不自然

---

# 🧠 你现在的 pipeline（我推测）

```text
翻译 → TTS生成 → forced alignment → 拉伸/裁剪
```

👉 问题就在这里：

* TTS 是“自由节奏”
* alignment 是“强制贴时间轴”

👉 两者冲突 = 抖动感（你现在听到的）

---

# ❗核心问题拆解

## 1️⃣ TTS 本身节奏不稳定

像
👉 IndexTTS

这种模型：

* 每次生成的语速是**漂的**
* 停顿位置是**随机的**
* 情绪会影响节奏

👉 所以你后面对齐：

* 有的段被压缩（听起来快）
* 有的段被拉长（听起来拖）

---

## 2️⃣ forced alignment 的本质

工具（如 MFA / Whisper align）在做的是：

👉 “把音频硬匹配到文本”

但它不会：

* ❌ 改善节奏
* ❌ 优化语速
* ❌ 保证自然感

👉 它只是“标时间”，不是“修声音”

---

# 🔥 正确思路（关键转变）

你要从：

❌ **Audio → Fit Timeline**

变成：

✅ **Text → Fit Timeline → Generate Audio**

👉 核心一句话：

> **时长控制要发生在 TTS 之前，而不是之后**

---

# 🚀 给你一套可落地的优化方案（重点）

## ✅ 方案一：分段 + 目标时长驱动（最有效）

### Step 1：切句（非常关键）

```json
[
  { "text": "Hello everyone", "start": 0.0, "end": 2.3 },
  { "text": "Welcome back", "start": 2.3, "end": 4.0 }
]
```

---

### Step 2：每段计算目标长度

```python
duration = end - start
target_chars = duration * 3.5  # 中文经验值
```

---

### Step 3：翻译时直接约束长度

```text
Translate to Chinese.
Keep it within ~8 characters.
Match a 2.3 second speaking duration.
```

👉 ⚠️ 这一步比 alignment 重要 10 倍

---

### Step 4：TTS 多采样（关键技巧🔥）

```python
candidates = [tts(text) for _ in range(3)]

best = min(candidates, key=lambda x: abs(len(x)-target_duration))
```

👉 选“最接近目标时长”的

---

### Step 5：只做轻微 stretch（≤10%）

用：

* ffmpeg `atempo`
* 或 phase vocoder

👉 原则：

> **只微调，不重塑**

---

## ✅ 方案二：插入“节奏控制符”（影视级技巧）

你可以在文本里加：

```text
嗯…… / 然后 / 那个 / （停顿）
```

例如：

```text
我觉得还可以 → 嗯……我觉得还可以
```

👉 用来：

* 填充时间
* 控制节奏
* 避免拉伸

---

## ✅ 方案三：放弃“整句对齐”，改“子句对齐”

不要对齐整句：

❌

```text
This is a long sentence that...
```

✅

```text
This is a long sentence /
that should be split /
for better timing
```

👉 每段独立控制 → 稳定性暴涨

---

## ⚠️ 你现在的问题本质总结

👉 你在做：

> “用对齐工具修正生成误差”

👉 正确做法是：

> “在生成前消灭误差”

---

# 🧠 进阶（你这个项目可以做很强）

如果你想做到更专业一点（接近 TikTok / YouTube dubbing）：

### 🔥 可以加一个模块：

👉 **duration predictor（时长预测器）**

输入：

```text
文本 + 音色
```

输出：

```text
预计时长
```

👉 用来反向调节文本长度

---

# 🎯 一句话结论

👉 你现在“时快时慢”的原因不是 alignment 技术问题，而是：

> ❌ “生成不可控 + 后期强对齐”
> → ✅ 应该变成 “生成前就对齐时长”

---

# 如果你要继续优化（我可以帮你做更狠的）

我可以给你一版：

👉 **“工业级配音 pipeline（带代码）”**

包括：

* Python SDK（你之前说要做）
* REST API（OpenAI风格）
* iOS 实时配音方案
* 音色克隆 + 时长控制一体化

甚至可以做到：
👉 “一句话自动生成 perfectly aligned dubbing”

要不要我直接帮你把这套系统设计到可以开工的程度？

---


**OpenAI Codex**

你这段判断**大方向是对的**，而且和你现在遇到的现象高度一致。  
我给你更精确定性：

- 不是单一 `forced alignment` 坏了。
- 是**两段链路目标冲突**：`TTS自由生成` vs `后处理硬贴时间轴`。
- 再叠加你项目里已有问题（字幕清洗、分组/切句不稳、长静音切段），就会出现你听到的“忽快忽慢、漏音、卡顿”。

**我建议定的原则（你这项目可直接落地）：**
- 以“句子 start 对齐”为硬约束。
- `end` 不强制硬贴，允许自然结束（超出再看是否可借后续静音补偿）。
- 压缩/拉伸只允许小幅（比如 ±10%）。
- 大幅不匹配时，不要硬压；走“重生成候选 + 选最接近时长”的路径。

**所以结论一句话：**
你贴的这版思路是对的，核心就是把“时长控制前移到生成前”，后处理只做轻微修正，不再当“救火队”。

如果你点头，我下一步就按这个给你出一版**项目内可执行的改造方案（先方案，不动代码）**：逐步替换当前链路，明确每一步改哪个函数、保留哪些兼容分支。

---


**OpenAI Codex**

你这个“还是零碎片段，不是一句一句”的现象，**我已经定位到根因了**，不是你错觉。

**现状定性（有出处）**
- `dub_pipeline` 默认就是按组跑，不是逐句：`--grouped-synthesis` 默认 `true`，`--force-fit-timing` 默认 `true`（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:3968`, `tools/dub_pipeline.py:3969`）。
- 只有“上传字幕且标记为 translated”才会强制改成逐句：`input_srt_is_translated` 分支里才把 grouped 关掉（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:4174`）。
- Web 启动参数里目前**没显式传** `--grouped-synthesis false`，所以大多数链路仍沿用默认分组（`src/subtitle_maker/dubbing_cli_api.py:760`）。
- 你看到 `group_xxx_raw_part000.wav` 是 index-tts 内部分块产物：先按 token/标点拆 chunk（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:2223`），再逐块合成并 concat（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:2411`）。
- 这些 `*_partNNN.wav` 只在 concat 后才清理；如果中途失败会残留（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:2416`）。
- 长视频且上传字幕时，还会先做视频分段，再把字幕按分段边界裁切，跨边界句子会被切碎（`tools/dub_long_video.py:845`, `tools/dub_long_video.py:1041`）。

**所以为什么会坏**
- 不是单点 forced alignment 坏了。  
- 是“默认分组合成 + index-tts内部再分块 + 分段裁字幕”三层叠加，最终看起来就不是“一句一句”。

如果你确认这段分析没问题，我下一段就给你“只改主链路、不打补丁感”的修复方案（先方案，不动代码）。

我现在觉得字幕的处理会直接影响后面的处理，如果我在字幕阶段直接处理好“一句一句”和对应的时间段，是不是事情就变简单了？

---


**OpenAI Codex**

对，**你这个判断是对的**。  
把“一句一句 + 时间轴”在字幕阶段定死，后面会简单很多。

- 后面 TTS 只做：`按句生成 -> 贴回 start`  
- 不再需要大量“分组再拆、强行拉伸、事后救火”
- 可控性最高，问题最少

你可以把链路收敛成：

1) 字幕阶段产出“标准句单元”（稳定切句 + 干净文本）  
2) 每句固定 `start/end`（允许 end 软约束）  
3) TTS 按句生成，优先自然时长  
4) 只做小幅度时长修正（超阈值再重生成，不硬压）  
5) 合成时严格按句 `start` 回贴

一句话：  
**前置把字幕做好 = 后面配音/对齐/合成都会变成机械稳定流程。**

**OpenAI Codex**

会这样处理，保证后面配音稳定：

- 先把每行解析成 `start + text`：`[m:ss] 文本`
- `start` 转秒；`end` 用**下一行 start**，最后一行用音频总时长
- 情绪标签（如 `[laughter] [applause] [music]`）标记为 `non_speech`，**不进TTS**
- 文本清洗：去 HTML、去纯情绪括号段、去 `♪` 相关内容
- 生成“句单元”后锁定时间轴：每句固定 `start`，`end` 软约束（可自然收尾）
- TTS 只按句生成并回贴到 `start`；仅做小幅时长修正，不做大压缩
- 合成时：`non_speech` 区域保留原声，其它区域替换为配音

你的这份数据尤其要加两条规则：
- 它只有起始时间，没有结束时间，所以必须用“下一句起点推 end”
- 像 `[14:14] [applause]` 这种整句情绪行要直接跳过配音

示例（前两行）：
- `[0:04] ...` → `start=4.0, end=11.0`
- `[0:11] ...` → `start=11.0, end=23.0`

这样前面把“句+时间”定准，后面链路会简单很多。  
如果你要，我下一步给你这个格式的**标准化规则清单（可直接落到代码）**。

**OpenAI Codex**

对，你说得对。规则应改成：

- `^\[\d{1,2}:\d{2}\]` 这类方括号是**时间戳**，必须保留并解析。
- 只有方括号里是文本词（如 `[laughter] [applause] [music]`）才当**情绪标签**跳过配音。
- 同一行可同时有时间戳和情绪词：先取时间，再清理情绪词。
- 若清理后文本为空，则该行标记 `non_speech`，不进 TTS。 

你这个边界非常关键，按这个做才不会误删正常时间。

**OpenAI Codex**

完全对，时间解析要支持两种：

- `[\d{1,2}:\d{2}]` → `MM:SS`
- `[\d{1,2}:\d{2}:\d{2}]` → `HH:MM:SS`（你说的 `[02:17:39]`）

建议统一规则：

- 只把**纯数字冒号格式**方括号当时间：`^\[(\d{1,2}:)?\d{1,2}:\d{2}\]`
- 像 `[laughter] [applause]` 这种非数字时间方括号才当情绪标签
- 先解析行首时间，再清理后续情绪标签，避免误删时间戳

这样 1 小时以上内容也稳。

**User**

这一步把句子和start-end时间分好了，下一步如何控制TTS生成的配音落在区间之内呢

---


**OpenAI Codex**

用这套就稳，而且不容易漏音：

- **硬约束 `start`**：每句配音一律从 `start` 贴回去（绝不漂移）。
- **软约束 `end`**：先不硬裁；只要不撞下一句 `next_start - guard` 就允许自然结束。
- **目标时长**：`target = end - start`，`effective_target = min(next_start - start - guard, target + 可借静音)`。
- **多候选生成**：同一句 TTS 生成 2~3 次，选最接近 `effective_target` 的。
- **小幅修正**：仅当超差时做轻微变速（如 0.92~1.08），禁止大压缩/大拉伸。
- **超限兜底**：仍超出就“到上限裁尾（带短淡出）”，并记录 `manual_review`。
- **防碰撞贴轨**：合成前再做一次检查，若当前句会覆盖下句 `start`，只截到安全点，不动下句。
- **最后一句特例**：上限用“音频总时长 - start”，不要按下一句算。

一句话：**start 严格对齐，end 采用“自然优先 + 安全上限”**。这样既不串句，也不容易漏尾音。

---


**OpenAI Codex**

对，你这个判断很准。  
你们现在不是“完全没做时长控制”，而是**上游句单元质量差**导致后面再怎么控也难听。

核心是这三个连锁：

- 分句不稳（太长/太碎/跨语义断开）
- 时间窗不稳（句子窗口与真实停顿不匹配）
- 文本不稳（口语重复、情绪标签、ASR噪声没清干净）

结果就是：
- 句太长 → 被压缩，发快
- 句太短或窗口太窄 → 截尾漏音
- 句边界错 → 听感卡顿、节奏撕裂

所以根因排序应该是：
**先修字幕句单元质量（最高优先） > 再调TTS时长策略 > 最后才是后处理对齐**。

你这次方向是对的：先把“句+时间”做成高质量标准件，后面自然会稳很多。

## 1️⃣ 当前仓库（IndexTTS v1.x）

你给的这个仓库：
👉 index-tts

👉 **默认版本（IndexTTS / 1.5）**

* ❌ **不能精确控制音频时长**
* 生成长度基本由：

  * 文本长度
  * 语速（隐式）
  * 模型推理过程
    共同决定

👉 也就是说：

* 你**不能写“生成10秒音频”**
* 只能通过：

  * 改文本长度
  * 改 prompt 风格（慢/快）
    进行**间接控制**

---

## 2️⃣ 新一代（IndexTTS2）——关键点

仓库里已经提到新模型：

👉 **IndexTTS2（论文/新版本）**

它确实解决了你问的这个问题👇

### ✔ 支持“时长控制”，但方式是：

不是直接输入秒数，而是：

### 方法A：控制 token 数量

* 你可以指定生成多少“语音 token”
* token ≈ 时间长度（间接映射）

👉 本质：

> token 数量 ↑ → 音频更长 ([GitHub][1])

---

### 方法B：倍率控制（类似 0.75x / 1.25x）

官方 demo 里展示：

* 0.75x（更短）
* 1.0x（正常）
* 1.25x（更长） ([IndexTTS][2])

---

### 方法C：自由生成（不控制）

* 完全交给模型（最自然）
* 不保证时长

---

## ⚠️ 但有一个坑（非常重要）

虽然论文和说明写了：

👉 “支持精确时长控制”

但仓库里明确说：

> **这个功能“还没在当前 release 里启用”** ([GitHub][1])

也就是说：

| 能力       | 状态             |
| -------- | -------------- |
| 理论支持     | ✅              |
| demo展示   | ✅              |
| 开源代码可直接用 | ❌（暂未 fully 开启） |

---

## 3️⃣ 如果你想“控制音乐/语音时长”，现实建议

### ✅ 现在能用的方案

1. **最简单（当前仓库）**

   * 控制文本长度
   * 加停顿词（例如：……、逗号）
   * 调语速 prompt（slow / calm）

---

### 🚀 更工程化一点

你可以做：

```text
目标：10秒音频
→ 估算字数（例如：中文≈4字/秒）
→ 生成约40字文本
→ 用 TTS 生成
→ 不够就补句子
```

---

### 🔥 真正精准控制（推荐路线）

如果你是做产品（你之前说要做 iOS / AI agent）：

👉 等 or 改造 IndexTTS2：

* 控制 token 数
* 或做一个：

  ```
  seconds → token 映射函数
  ```

---

## 4️⃣ 总结一句话

👉 这个仓库：

* ❌ 现在不能直接控制时长
* ✅ 下一代（IndexTTS2）已经支持，但还没完全开放

建议我们落地为：
1. 先把字幕句单元做稳（你前面说的那套）。  
2. 生成前做时长预算（按句估算长度，必要时先改写长短）。  
3. 每句 TTS 多候选，选最接近目标时长。  
4. 后处理只做小幅修正（比如 ±8%），避免硬压硬拉。

**目标**
- 把主链路改成：`先稳定句单元(句+start/end) -> 再TTS候选选择 -> 最后轻微修正`
- 彻底降低“分组撕裂、硬压缩漏音、节奏忽快忽慢”

**方案（按模块）**
- `tools/dub_pipeline.py::load_or_transcribe_subtitles`
  - 新增“句单元标准化层”：统一清洗、切句、补全 `end`（下一句 `start`），最后一句用媒体总时长。
- `tools/dub_pipeline.py::synthesize_segments`
  - 固定走逐句主流程（把“逐句”变主分支，分组变可选兼容）。
  - 每句先做目标时长预算：`target=end-start`，`effective_target=target+可借静音`（你们已有 `compute_effective_target_duration`）。
  - TTS 做 2~3 个候选，选最接近 `effective_target` 的。
  - 超差仅允许小幅修正（建议 ±8%），超限再进入 manual_review。
- `tools/dub_pipeline.py::synthesize_segments_grouped`
  - 降级为兼容模式（默认不走），避免再出现 group 节奏撕裂主导链路。
- `src/subtitle_maker/dubbing_cli_api.py::start_auto_dubbing`
  - Web 端显式传“逐句模式”参数，不再依赖默认值（当前默认 `grouped-synthesis=true`）。
- `tools/dub_long_video.py::clip_subtitles_for_segment`
  - 保持“按段裁字幕”，但要求先做全局句单元标准化，减少跨段断裂。

**为什么这样定（代码依据）**
- 当前默认就是分组：`--grouped-synthesis=true`（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:3969`）
- 只有“上传 translated 字幕”才强制逐句（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:4174`）
- Web 目前没强制传逐句（`src/subtitle_maker/dubbing_cli_api.py:760` 附近）
- index-tts 当前是分块合成后拼接，不是秒级硬控（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:2223`, `tools/dub_pipeline.py:2411`）
- 现有时长控制主要在后处理（`tools/dub_pipeline.py:2676`）

**验收口径**
- 句级产物全部有 `start/end/text/type(speech/non_speech)`
- 合成后无跨句覆盖；最后一句不被错误压缩
- `manual_review` 比例显著下降
- `group_*` 产物不再是主路径，用户看到以句为单位结果

  - 决策：每实例保存自己的 `pollInterval`，任务结束/失败时必清理

**User**

那怎么选择最好的呢？标准是什么

---


**OpenAI Codex**

当前“最好”的标准是：**和目标时长最接近**。

- 目标时长：`effective_target_duration`（句子窗口 + 可借静音）
- 每次尝试算误差：`abs(actual - effective_target)`
- 误差更小就更新为 best（代码就是这个比较逻辑）
- 若误差已小于阈值（`delta_pass_ms`），直接定稿
- 否则再走小幅 `atempo` 修正后再比较
- 最终把 best 复制成 `seg_xxxx.wav`

一句话：现在是“**时长误差最小优先**”，不是音质打分优先。

**User**

**OpenAI Codex**

不是“没学情绪表达”导致的，核心是**时长预算错配**：

- 你的字幕句子窗口本身就不自然（有的给太长，有的给太短）
- V2 现在禁了重写文本，只能原句硬配这个窗口
- index-tts 本质不是“按秒生成”，同一句自然时长会漂

所以会出现：
- 12 秒窗口里一句短中文 → 只能说到 5~6 秒（偏短）
- 1 秒窗口里一句完整话 → 怎么都超时（偏长）

情绪参考（emo/ref）只影响语气、停顿风格，**不负责精准把句子拉到指定秒数**。  
这次的巨大误差，本质是“字幕时间窗与文本负载不匹配”，不是情绪模型失效。

**OpenAI Codex**

你说得对，我刚那句不准确。  
这不是“你字幕有问题”，而是**我们当前选择策略太单一**。

现在实际是：
- 主要按时长误差选 best
- 没有把“情绪一致性”作为显式评分项

所以你要的目标应该改成：**双目标优化**  
`情绪一致 + 时长接近`，不是只看时长。

我建议直接改成这套打分（可落地）：

- 每句生成 2~4 个候选
- 对每个候选打两类分：
  - `duration_score`：`|actual - effective_target|`
  - `emotion_score`：和参考音色片段的 prosody 相似度（能量包络/语速节拍/停顿分布）
- 总分：`score = 0.55*duration + 0.45*emotion`（可调）
- 约束：
  - 若超出硬上限（撞下一句）直接淘汰
  - 轻微 atempo 仅作最后微调（±8%）
- 输出：
  - 保存每句候选评分到日志，方便你复盘“为何选了这句”

这样才是你要的“两个方向同时逼近”。

你的意思是重写翻译字幕，控制token，从而控制时长吗

**OpenAI Codex**

不是“只靠重写翻译”。

现在是三层一起做：

- **第一层**：保留你字幕语义和情绪，不乱改文案（V2 默认不重写）。
- **第二层**：同一句生成多个候选（token/随机性会影响时长），选“时长+情绪”综合最优。
- **第三层**：只做小幅微调（±8%），避免硬压硬拉。

所以控制时长的主手段是“**候选选择 + 轻微修正**”，不是强行重写文本。  
只有在你明确开启时，才会用“重写短一点/长一点”作为兜底。

**User**

重配后如何合成到最后结果

---


**OpenAI Codex**

重配后这样合成（不全量重跑）：

- 定位被改句对应的 `segment_xxxx`
- 只在该 segment 内重配该句，更新该段 `dubbed_vocals.wav`（和可选 `dubbed_mix.wav`）
- 重写该段 `manifest.json` 的句记录与统计
- 回到 batch 层：
  - 只替换该段在全时轴的位置
  - 重新生成 `final/dubbed_vocals_full.wav`
  - 再用 BGM 重建 `final/dubbed_mix_full.wav`
  - 更新 `final/translated_full.srt` / `final/dubbed_final_full.srt`
- 最后刷新 `batch_manifest.json` 路径与时间戳

<permissions instructions>
Filesystem sandboxing defines which files can be read or written. `sandbox_mode` is `workspace-write`: The sandbox permits reading files, and editing files in `cwd` and `writable_roots`. Editing files in other directories requires approval. Network access is restricted.
# Escalation Requests

Commands are run outside the sandbox if they are approved by the user, or match an existing rule that allows it to run unrestricted. The command string is split into independent command segments at shell control operators, including but not limited to:

- Pipes: |
- Logical operators: &&, ||
- Command separators: ;
- Subshell boundaries: (...), $(...)

Each resulting segment is evaluated independently for sandbox restrictions and approval requirements.

Example:

git pull | tee output.txt

This is treated as two command segments:

["git", "pull"]

["tee", "output.txt"]

Commands that use more advanced shell features like redirection (>, >>, <), substitutions ($(...), ...), environment variables (FOO=bar), or wildcard patterns (*, ?) will not be evaluated against rules, to limit the scope of what an approved rule allows.

## How to request escalation

IMPORTANT: To request approval to execute a command that will require escalated privileges:

- Provide the `sandbox_permissions` parameter with the value `"require_escalated"`
- Include a short question asking the user if they want to allow the action in `justification` parameter. e.g. "Do you want to download and install dependencies for this project?"
- Optionally suggest a `prefix_rule` - this will be shown to the user with an option to persist the rule approval for future sessions.

If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with "require_escalated". ALWAYS proceed to use the `justification` parameter - do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.

## When to request escalation

While commands are running inside the sandbox, here are some scenarios that will require escalation outside the sandbox:

- You need to run a command that writes to a directory that requires it (e.g. running tests that write to /var)
- You need to run a GUI app (e.g., open/xdg-open/osascript) to open browsers or files.
- If you run a command that is important to solving the user's query, but it fails because of sandboxing or with a likely sandbox-related network error (for example DNS/host resolution, registry/index access, or dependency download failure), rerun the command with `require_escalated`. ALWAYS proceed to use the `sandbox_permissions` and `justification` parameters. do not message the user before requesting approval for the command.
- You are about to take a potentially destructive action such as an `rm` or `git reset` that the user did not explicitly ask for.
- Be judicious with escalating, but if completing the user's request requires it, you should do so - don't try and circumvent approvals by using other tools.

## prefix_rule guidance

When choosing a `prefix_rule`, request one that will allow you to fulfill similar requests from the user in the future without re-requesting escalation. It should be categorical and reasonably scoped to similar capabilities. You should rarely pass the entire command into `prefix_rule`.

### Banned prefix_rules 
Avoid requesting overly broad prefixes that the user would be ill-advised to approve. For example, do not request ["python3"], ["python", "-"], or other similar prefixes that would allow arbitrary scripting.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule argument for destructive commands like rm.
NEVER provide a prefix_rule if your command uses a heredoc or herestring. 

### Examples
Good examples of prefixes:
- ["npm", "run", "dev"]
- ["gh", "pr", "check"]
- ["cargo", "test"]

### How to use skills
- Discovery: The list above is the skills available in this session (name + description + file path). Skill bodies live on disk at the listed paths.
- Trigger rules: If the user names a skill (with `$SkillName` or plain text) OR the task clearly matches a skill's description shown above, you must use that skill for that turn. Multiple mentions mean use them all. Do not carry skills across turns unless re-mentioned.
- Missing/blocked: If a named skill isn't in the list or the path can't be read, say so briefly and continue with the best fallback.
- How to use a skill (progressive disclosure):
  1) After deciding to use a skill, open its `SKILL.md`. Read only enough to follow the workflow.
  2) When `SKILL.md` references relative paths (e.g., `scripts/foo.py`), resolve them relative to the skill directory listed above first, and only consider other paths if needed.
  3) If `SKILL.md` points to extra folders such as `references/`, load only the specific files needed for the request; don't bulk-load everything.
  4) If `scripts/` exist, prefer running or patching them instead of retyping large code blocks.
  5) If `assets/` or templates exist, reuse them instead of recreating from scratch.
- Coordination and sequencing:
  - If multiple skills apply, choose the minimal set that covers the request and state the order you'll use them.
  - Announce which skill(s) you're using and why (one short line). If you skip an obvious skill, say why.
- Context hygiene:
  - Keep context small: summarize long sections instead of pasting them; only load extra files when needed.
  - Avoid deep reference-chasing: prefer opening only files directly linked from `SKILL.md` unless you're blocked.
  - When variants exist (frameworks, providers, domains), pick only the relevant reference file(s) and note that choice.
- Safety and fallback: If a skill can't be applied cleanly (missing files, unclear instructions), state the issue, pick the next-best approach, and continue.

### 源头问题（不是 TTS 本身）
**数据单一真相（single source of truth）被破坏了**，现在有三份“翻译文本”在跑：
1. `final/translated_full.srt`
2. `segment_jobs/*/subtitles/translated.srt`
3. `segment manifest.json` 里的 `translated_text`

一旦其中一份变成源字幕，后面“保存并重配”就会拿错文本去配音。

---

### 你现在出现“重配成源字幕”的真正触发点
不是合成阶段，通常是**保存阶段先污染了译文文件**：

- 前端“保存并重配”提交的是整页 textarea（全量），不是严格 delta  
- 后端会把提交内容写回 `translated.srt`
- 重配读取的正是这个 `translated.srt`
- 所以一旦提交内容里混进源字幕，后面必然配源字幕

---

### 一句话结论
**问题源头是“审阅读写链路的数据一致性设计错误”，不是 TTS 对齐算法。**

同意，必须双标准把关：**情绪表达** + **时长控制**，而且要有硬门槛，不是“尽量”。

- **文本一致性（硬门槛）**：TTS 输入必须等于你编辑后的句子文本。
- **时长门槛**：优先命中 `target = end-start(+可借静音)`；超阈值才轻微 `atempo`（限制在小范围）。
- **情绪门槛**：候选里按“时长误差 + 韵律距离”联合评分，取最优，不只看时长。
- **失败策略**：不达标不硬贴，标记 `manual_review`，禁止静默回退成源字幕。
- **可审计**：每句记录 `input_text / target_sec / actual_sec / prosody_distance / selection_score`，可追溯。

**OpenAI Codex**

V2 当前统一标准是：

- 候选评分：`selection_score = 0.55 * duration_error_ratio + 0.45 * prosody_distance`
- `duration_error_ratio = |actual - effective_target| / effective_target`
- `prosody_distance`：候选与参考音频在能量/停顿/起伏等韵律特征的距离
- 选择策略：每句多候选里取 `selection_score` 最小者，不只看时长
- 记录字段：`selection_score`、`duration_error_ratio`、`prosody_distance` 都写入段记录，便于审计

对应代码在 `tools/dub_pipeline.py` 的 `synthesize_segments -> evaluate_candidate`。

**OpenAI Codex**

你说“详细一点”，我直接用**一条句子从审阅到重配**的真实链路讲清楚：

### 你期望的链路（正确）
1. 你在“逐句审阅”里看到一句翻译：`A`
2. 你改成：`B`
3. 点“保存并重配改动句”
4. 后端应该把 `B` 写入该句的 `translated.srt`
5. TTS 输入必须是 `B`
6. 最终音频就是 `B` 的配音

---

### 现在为什么会变成源字幕（错误链路）
问题出在 `resume` 重配时的“字幕选择顺序”：

1. 后端虽然收到了 `--input-srt-kind translated` 和 `translated.srt`
2. 但代码先去复用 `source.srt`（源字幕）作为 `subtitles`
3. 后面又在 translated 分支里，用 `subtitles` 的文本重建 `translated_lines`
4. 这一步把你的翻译文本覆盖成了 source 文本
5. TTS 最终拿的是 source 内容，所以你听到“配回源字幕”

---

### 为什么你会感觉“我明明改的是配音文本”
你感觉完全正确，因为前端编辑框确实是翻译文本。  
问题不是你改错，是后端在重配阶段把文本源换掉了。

---

### 这和你说的“V2统一标准”关系
你说的标准（`时长误差 + 韵律距离`联合评分）是**候选选优**阶段。  
而现在这个 bug 是更早的**输入文本被换源**问题：

- 输入错了（source）  
- 后面的评分再好也只能在“错误文本”里选最优

所以必须先保证：**重配输入文本就是你编辑后的 translated 文本**。

---

### 一句话结论
你这次的问题本质不是评分策略没生效，而是：
**在 save-and-redub 的 resume 链路里，translated 模式被 source 复用逻辑污染了。**
